    *   **Limited Model Selection:** While the paper evaluates several open-source LLMs, it would be valuable to extend the evaluation to other popular LLMs.
    *   **Limited Analysis of the word frequency signal:** Though the shadow model attack with word frequency seems to work, the exact properties of the words that lead to the prediction is not explicitly studied.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field by raising awareness of the property inference vulnerability in LLMs and providing a framework for future research.  It should stimulate the development of new defense mechanisms and influence the design of privacy-preserving fine-tuning techniques for LLMs.

*Score: 7*

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by addressing a previously underexplored vulnerability in LLMs. The creation of the PropInfer benchmark is a significant strength, providing a foundation for future research. The tailored attacks and empirical validation are also well-executed. However, the limited exploration of defenses, the scope of properties and the model selection are weaknesses that prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. While the techniques for attacking are not entirely new, the adaptation to the LLM is valuable. The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by prompting further research into defense mechanisms and privacy-preserving techniques.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoGEEval++: A Multi-Level and Multi-Geospatial-Modality Automated Evaluation Framework for Large Language Models in Geospatial Code Generation on Google Earth Engine
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10365v1)
- **Authors**: Shuyang Hou, Zhangxiao Shen, Huayi Wu, Haoyue Jiao, Ziqi Liu, Lutong Xie, Chang Liu, Jianyuan Liang, Yaxian Qing, Xiaopu Zhang, Dehua Peng, Zhipeng Gui, Xuefeng Guan
- **Abstract**: Geospatial code generation is becoming a key frontier in integrating artificial intelligence with geo-scientific analysis, yet standardised automated evaluation tools for this task remain absent. This study presents AutoGEEval++, an enhanced framework building on AutoGEEval, and the first automated assessment system for large language models (LLMs) generating geospatial code on Google Earth Engine (GEE). It supports diverse data modalities and varying task complexities. Built on the GEE Python API, AutoGEEval++ features a benchmark dataset-AutoGEEval++-Bench-with 6,365 test cases across 26 data types and three task categories: unit, combo, and theme tests. It includes a submission programme and a judge module to realise an end-to-end automated evaluation pipeline from code generation to execution-based validation. The framework adopts multi-dimensional metrics-accuracy, resource usage, run-time efficiency, and error types-balancing hallucination control and efficiency, and enabling boundary testing and error pattern analysis. Using AutoGEEval++, we evaluate 24 state-of-the-art LLMs (as of June 2025), including general-purpose, reasoning-enhanced, code-centric, and geoscience-specific models. Results reveal clear performance, stability, and error differences across task types, model designs, and deployment settings, confirming AutoGEEval++'s practical value and scalability in vertical-domain code generation. This work establishes the first standardised evaluation protocol and foundational benchmark for GEE-based LLM code generation, providing a unified basis for performance comparison and a methodological framework for systematic, domain-specific code evaluation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AutoGEEval++, an enhanced automated evaluation framework for large language models (LLMs) generating geospatial code for Google Earth Engine (GEE).  It builds upon the existing AutoGEEval framework by extending support for diverse geospatial data modalities and multiple levels of task complexity. AutoGEEval++ includes a benchmark dataset (AutoGEEval++-Bench) comprising 6,365 test cases across 26 geospatial data types and three task categories (unit test, combo test, and theme test). The framework integrates a submission and judge program, enabling an end-to-end evaluation pipeline from code generation to execution-based verification. It utilizes multi-dimensional metrics, including accuracy, resource consumption, run-time efficiency, and error types. The framework is used to evaluate 24 state-of-the-art LLMs, revealing their performance characteristics and error tendencies. The authors position the framework as a standardized evaluation protocol and foundational resource for GEE-based LLM code generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advance over existing work in automated LLM evaluation for geospatial code generation. AutoGEEval++ fills a critical gap by providing a standardized and comprehensive framework for evaluating LLMs on a specific vertical domain. The extension of AutoGEEval to encompass multi-level tasks, multimodal data, and a more extensive benchmark dataset is a key contribution. The systematic evaluation of a diverse set of LLMs provides valuable insights into their capabilities and limitations in this domain.

*   **Significance:** This work has the potential to significantly impact the fields of AI, geospatial analysis, and remote sensing. It establishes a unified benchmark for comparing LLM performance and provides a methodological paradigm for systematic evaluation from natural language to domain-specific code. This can facilitate further research and development of geospatial AI applications, including automated geospatial analysis, resource management, and environmental monitoring. The provided dataset and framework contribute directly to improving the trustworthiness of LLMs in geospatial contexts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** The framework is well-designed, incorporating multiple levels of task complexity, data types, and evaluation metrics.

    *   **Automated Pipeline:** The automated pipeline, from code generation to execution and evaluation, ensures reproducibility and scalability.

    *   **Benchmark Dataset:** The AutoGEEval++-Bench dataset is a valuable resource for the community.

    *   **Systematic LLM Evaluation:** The systematic evaluation of a diverse set of LLMs provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.

    *   **Practical Relevance:** The framework is based on the widely used GEE platform, making it relevant for real-world applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Focus:** While the evaluation covers a range of models, it focuses primarily on general-purpose and code generation models. Further investigation of LLMs specifically tailored for geospatial code generation would strengthen the study.

    *   **Dataset Bias**: The dependence on GEE as a platform could introduce bias to the data. If many of the code snippets came from Javascript instead of python, and the GEE api is more mature in Javascript, then python outputs might be worse.

    *   **Single Turn Generation:** The evaluation is limited to single-turn generation, neglecting multi-turn dialogue and interactive debugging scenarios.

    *   **Limited Data Sources**: The data sources may present a selection bias based on the most frequent patterns, instead of including more rare but possibly useful examples.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work is likely to be widely adopted by researchers and practitioners working on geospatial AI. It will serve as a benchmark for comparing new LLMs and a guide for developing more effective evaluation methodologies.

*   **Score Rationale**: While the paper presents a solid contribution with a valuable framework and dataset, the weaknesses noted above prevent it from reaching the highest score. It is a significant advancement over previous research but has potential for further expansion and refinement.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Revisiting Transformers with Insights from Image Filtering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10371v1)
- **Authors**: Laziz U. Abdullaev, Maksim Tkachenko, Tan M. Nguyen
- **Abstract**: The self-attention mechanism, a cornerstone of Transformer-based state-of-the-art deep learning architectures, is largely heuristic-driven and fundamentally challenging to interpret. Establishing a robust theoretical foundation to explain its remarkable success and limitations has therefore become an increasingly prominent focus in recent research. Some notable directions have explored understanding self-attention through the lens of image denoising and nonparametric regression. While promising, existing frameworks still lack a deeper mechanistic interpretation of various architectural components that enhance self-attention, both in its original formulation and subsequent variants. In this work, we aim to advance this understanding by developing a unifying image processing framework, capable of explaining not only the self-attention computation itself but also the role of components such as positional encoding and residual connections, including numerous later variants. We also pinpoint potential distinctions between the two concepts building upon our framework, and make effort to close this gap. We introduce two independent architectural modifications within transformers. While our primary objective is interpretability, we empirically observe that image processing-inspired modifications can also lead to notably improved accuracy and robustness against data contamination and adversaries across language and vision tasks as well as better long sequence understanding.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary, critical evaluation, and novelty assessment of the paper.

**Summary:**

This paper presents a novel perspective on Transformers, interpreting them through the lens of image processing algorithms, specifically image filtering. The authors establish connections between self-attention, positional encoding, and residual connections with classical image filtering techniques like bilateral filtering and Non-Local Means. They theoretically demonstrate that self-attention can be viewed as a form of weighted least squares denoising and propose modifications, such as Bilateral Self-Attention (BSA) and a generalized residual connection (GRC) inspired by boosting in image processing, aimed at improving accuracy, robustness, and long sequence stability. They provide empirical support for their theoretical claims across language, vision, and long-range arena tasks. The authors argue that their framework provides a more interpretable and adaptable view of Transformers, paving the way for novel architectural improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Perspective:** The paper offers a refreshing and insightful perspective on Transformers by connecting them to well-established image processing techniques. This connection provides a more intuitive understanding of the inner workings of self-attention.
    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** The paper provides rigorous theoretical justifications for its claims, linking self-attention to weighted least squares denoising, residual connections to SNR boosting, and analyzing the stability implications of different attention mechanisms.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The proposed modifications, BSA and GRC, show promising empirical results across various tasks, suggesting the practical utility of the proposed framework. It achieves increased robustness across several experiments.
    *   **Improved Interpretability:** By relating Transformer components to familiar image processing concepts, the paper enhances the interpretability of these complex models.
    *   **Discussion of MLP Layers** The paper briefly discusses the use of mixture-of-expert methods which further highlights the robustness of the approach to potential implementation choices in a network.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Empirical Evaluation Scope:** While the results are promising, the empirical evaluation could be more extensive. More thorough ablation studies (e.g. different bandwidth selections in the Bilateral Attention) would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Simplicity Assumption:** The authors begin with a simplified Transformer architecture by removing MLP blocks and work their way through the discussion. Although the rationale makes sense, it is important to discuss this limitation as early architectural decisions in Transformers have a large impact on model performance.
    *   **Limited Novelty (to Image Processing):** While the application to Transformers is novel, the underlying image processing concepts are well-established. The theoretical contribution lies in bridging the gap between the two fields rather than introducing entirely new image processing techniques.
    *   **Computational Complexity:** The modified self-attention mechanism adds additional parameters to training. Although it does not have an impact to inference costs, the impact is substantial to training time.

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the cross-disciplinary connection between Transformers and image processing. The authors synthesize existing concepts from both fields to create a novel framework for understanding and modifying Transformer architectures. The specific modifications, such as BSA and GRC, are also novel, although they are inspired by existing image processing techniques. The theoretical analysis of the stability implications of different attention mechanisms is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it:
    *   Offers a new way to think about Transformers, potentially leading to new architectural innovations.
    *   Provides a theoretical foundation for understanding the behavior of self-attention mechanisms.
    *   Suggests practical modifications that can improve the performance and robustness of Transformers.
    *   Potentially makes the design of Transformer architectures more intuitive and accessible to researchers familiar with image processing.

**Score and Justification:**

I assign the paper a score of **7.5**.

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates **significant novelty** in its cross-disciplinary approach, successfully bridging the gap between Transformers and image processing. The **theoretical rigor** is a major strength, providing a solid foundation for the proposed framework. The **empirical results** support the practical relevance of the approach. However, **the novelty of the image filtering concepts themselves is limited**, as is the **scope of the empirical evaluation**. The **additional training complexity** is a limitation, as well. While the impact is not at the top-end of novelty and is not a fundamental change to the state-of-the-art of Transformer development, it is a major leap forward in offering better interpretability of these models and offering more interpretable modification. As a result, it will have a medium-to-high impact in the field. The theoretical work is also well-derived and will likely be cited in other works on the subject.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MLLM-Based UI2Code Automation Guided by UI Layout Information
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10376v1)
- **Authors**: Fan Wu, Cuiyun Gao, Shuqing Li, Xin-Cheng Wen, Qing Liao
- **Abstract**: Converting user interfaces into code (UI2Code) is a crucial step in website development, which is time-consuming and labor-intensive. The automation of UI2Code is essential to streamline this task, beneficial for improving the development efficiency. There exist deep learning-based methods for the task; however, they heavily rely on a large amount of labeled training data and struggle with generalizing to real-world, unseen web page designs. The advent of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) presents potential for alleviating the issue, but they are difficult to comprehend the complex layouts in UIs and generate the accurate code with layout preserved. To address these issues, we propose LayoutCoder, a novel MLLM-based framework generating UI code from real-world webpage images, which includes three key modules: (1) Element Relation Construction, which aims at capturing UI layout by identifying and grouping components with similar structures; (2) UI Layout Parsing, which aims at generating UI layout trees for guiding the subsequent code generation process; and (3) Layout-Guided Code Fusion, which aims at producing the accurate code with layout preserved. For evaluation, we build a new benchmark dataset which involves 350 real-world websites named Snap2Code, divided into seen and unseen parts for mitigating the data leakage issue, besides the popular dataset Design2Code. Extensive evaluation shows the superior performance of LayoutCoder over the state-of-the-art approaches. Compared with the best-performing baseline, LayoutCoder improves 10.14% in the BLEU score and 3.95% in the CLIP score on average across all datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LayoutCoder, a novel MLLM-based framework for automating UI-to-Code conversion from real-world webpage images. Addressing the limitations of existing deep learning methods (reliance on large labeled datasets and poor generalization) and MLLMs (difficulty in comprehending complex layouts and preserving layout accuracy), LayoutCoder incorporates three key modules: Element Relation Construction (identifies and groups similar UI components), UI Layout Parsing (generates UI layout trees), and Layout-Guided Code Fusion (produces accurate code with layout preservation).  The paper presents a new benchmark dataset, Snap2Code, consisting of real-world websites, and evaluates LayoutCoder against state-of-the-art approaches, demonstrating superior performance. Human evaluations and analysis of performance across websites of varying complexity are also presented.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper is moderate to high. UI-to-code automation using deep learning is an established area.  The strength of this paper lies in its specific architecture and approach. The Element Relation Construction module, Layout Parsing, and Layout-Guided Code Fusion, while not entirely revolutionary concepts in isolation, are combined in a novel and effective manner to address the challenges of complex UI layouts.  The data generation to explicitly split "seen" and "unseen" web pages to mitigate data leakage is a significant contribution, a point often overlooked in this area.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is substantial. UI-to-code automation is a practical problem with considerable industrial relevance.  By improving the ability to handle complex real-world webpages and explicitly addressing the data leakage problem, this paper moves the field forward. If the approach translates well to practical usage, it could significantly reduce development time and improve efficiency. The creation of the Snap2Code dataset is itself a significant contribution, providing a valuable resource for future research.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Effective Architecture:** The modular design of LayoutCoder, with its three key modules, allows for a targeted approach to addressing the core challenges of UI-to-code automation.
    *   **Explicit Handling of Data Leakage:** The split of Snap2Code into "Seen" and "Unseen" portions addresses a critical issue often ignored in previous research.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides extensive experimental results, comparing LayoutCoder to several strong baselines on multiple datasets using both automated metrics and human evaluation. The complexity analysis provides valuable insights into the model's performance characteristics.
    *   **New Dataset:** The creation and use of the Snap2Code is an important contribution allowing for more rigorous testing by others.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on MLLMs:** The performance of LayoutCoder heavily relies on the capabilities of the underlying MLLM (GPT-40 and Claude 3.5), and it might not be directly transferable to environments with less powerful or differently trained models, or open-source alternatives. Although addressed by presenting results against fine-tuned models too.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:**  The described framework has several steps and components, making it a substantial undertaking to implement.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in UI-to-code automation by:

    *   Highlighting the importance of explicit layout parsing and handling data leakage.
    *   Providing a strong baseline and a valuable new dataset (Snap2Code) for future comparisons.
    *   Demonstrating the effectiveness of a modular approach to UI-to-code conversion.

*   **Areas For Improvement/Future Work:**

    *   Explore the use of more efficient open-source MLLMs to reduce costs.
    *   Investigate the integration of additional layout information, such as style guides or design principles.
    *   Develop techniques for handling dynamic webpage elements and user interactions.
    *   Further explore the limits of the system.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to a practical problem, UI-to-code automation. Its novelty is in the carefully designed architecture of LayoutCoder and the inclusion of key mechanisms to both enhance the fidelity of complex layout representations and reduce data leakage during training.  The comprehensive evaluation and new dataset increase the impact of the work. While there are limitations, like reliance on proprietary MLLMs and complexity of implementation, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. It’s not quite ground-breaking enough for a 9 or 10, as much of the individual modules are well-understood, but the combination is compelling, and the data split makes a real difference, providing a solid foundation for future research and potential practical applications.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Chance and Mass Interpretations of Probabilities in Markov Decision Processes (Extended Version)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10377v1)
- **Authors**: Yun Chen Tsai, Kittiphon Phalakarn, S. Akshay, Ichiro Hasuo
- **Abstract**: Markov decision processes (MDPs) are a popular model for decision-making in the presence of uncertainty. The conventional view of MDPs in verification treats them as state transformers with probabilities defined over sequences of states and with schedulers making random choices. An alternative view, especially well-suited for modeling dynamical systems, defines MDPs as distribution transformers with schedulers distributing probability masses. Our main contribution is a unified semantical framework that accommodates these two views and two new ones. These four semantics of MDPs arise naturally through identifying different sources of randomness in an MDP (namely schedulers, configurations, and transitions) and providing different ways of interpreting these probabilities (called the chance and mass interpretations). These semantics are systematically unified through a mathematical construct called chance-mass (CM) classifier. As another main contribution, we study a reachability problem in each of the two new semantics, demonstrating their hardness and providing two algorithms for solving them.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a unified semantic framework for Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) by identifying different sources of randomness (schedulers, configurations, transitions) and different ways of interpreting probabilities (chance and mass interpretations). This results in four distinct semantics for MDPs. The framework uses a "chance-mass (CM) classifier" to systematically unify these semantics. The paper then focuses on the reachability problem in the two new semantics introduced, demonstrating their hardness and providing two algorithms for solving them: an antichain-based algorithm and a template-based synthesis algorithm. The paper also includes a prototype implementation of these algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in the unified semantic framework that combines the conventional, chance-based view of MDPs with the distribution-transformer, mass-based view. While both views have been explored separately, the systematic identification of sources of randomness and interpretations of probabilities to generate four distinct semantics and then unify them using a CM classifier appears to be novel. The introduction of the two intermediate semantics (CS,MT) and (MS,CT) and the investigation of their properties is also a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work helps to clarify the relationship between different interpretations of MDPs, which is useful for both theoretical understanding and practical applications. The analysis of the reachability problem under the two new semantics exposes the subtleties of dealing with randomness in decision-making under uncertainty, highlighting the inherent complexity of the problem under distribution transformer semantics. The two algorithms proposed offer initial solutions for addressing these problems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Framework:** The CM classifier provides a clean and mathematically rigorous way to understand the different semantics.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper thoroughly examines the computational complexity of reachability for the new semantics.
    *   **Algorithmic Contributions:** The development of both an antichain-based and a template-based algorithm addresses the reachability problem from different angles.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The prototype demonstrates the feasibility of the proposed techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Practical Applicability of New Semantics:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the specific application scenarios where the new (CS,MT) and (MS,CT) semantics are best suited. The examples given are helpful, but further motivation for adopting these semantics in real-world problems would strengthen the paper. The description of each semantics could also be improved so to be more accessible.
    *   **Undecidability and Computational Complexity:** While the hardness results for reachability are important, they also limit the practical use of the framework in some cases. The developed algorithms are sound but incomplete for certain scenarios. Further, the algorithms require very particular set-ups (finitely generated montone sets) which limit the generality and scope.
    *   **Complexity of Algorithms**: The algorithms are rather complex and only apply to a small set of examples. They also rely on approximations and heuristics.
    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** The prototype implementation appears to be limited and the provided evaluation is quite small, only focusing on "small models." A more extensive empirical evaluation would provide a better sense of their performance and scalability. The experiments could also be improved by adding more details.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's unified framework could influence how researchers model and analyze MDPs. The identification of different semantic interpretations is valuable for understanding the relationship between various existing approaches. The hardness results for reachability in the new semantics are also useful for directing future research efforts towards approximation techniques or alternative problem formulations. The algorithms developed, while limited in scope, provide a starting point for further exploration.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a significant theoretical contribution in unifying different semantics of MDPs. The in-depth complexity analysis of reachability under the new semantics is also valuable. However, the limited practical applicability of these semantics and the limited scope of the algorithms prevent a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.FL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReconMOST: Multi-Layer Sea Temperature Reconstruction with Observations-Guided Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10391v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanyi Song, Pumeng Lyu, Ben Fei, Fenghua Ling, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: Accurate reconstruction of ocean is essential for reflecting global climate dynamics and supporting marine meteorological research. Conventional methods face challenges due to sparse data, algorithmic complexity, and high computational costs, while increasing usage of machine learning (ML) method remains limited to reconstruction problems at the sea surface and local regions, struggling with issues like cloud occlusion. To address these limitations, this paper proposes ReconMOST, a data-driven guided diffusion model framework for multi-layer sea temperature reconstruction. Specifically, we first pre-train an unconditional diffusion model using a large collection of historical numerical simulation data, enabling the model to attain physically consistent distribution patterns of ocean temperature fields. During the generation phase, sparse yet high-accuracy in-situ observational data are utilized as guidance points for the reverse diffusion process, generating accurate reconstruction results. Importantly, in regions lacking direct observational data, the physically consistent spatial distribution patterns learned during pre-training enable implicitly guided and physically plausible reconstructions. Our method extends ML-based SST reconstruction to a global, multi-layer setting, handling over 92.5% missing data while maintaining reconstruction accuracy, spatial resolution, and superior generalization capability. We pre-train our model on CMIP6 numerical simulation data and conduct guided reconstruction experiments on CMIP6 and EN4 analysis data. The results of mean squared error (MSE) values achieve 0.049 on guidance, 0.680 on reconstruction, and 0.633 on total, respectively, demonstrating the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework. Our source code is available at https://github.com/norsheep/ReconMOST.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReconMOST, a novel data-driven, guided diffusion model for reconstructing multi-layer sea temperature fields. It addresses the limitations of traditional and machine learning methods that struggle with sparse and irregular observational data and cloud occlusion. ReconMOST leverages a pre-trained unconditional diffusion model, trained on a large collection of historical numerical simulation data, to learn physically consistent distribution patterns of ocean temperature fields. During reconstruction, it uses sparse in-situ observations as guidance points for a reverse diffusion process, enabling accurate reconstruction even in data-scarce regions. The method extends ML-based SST reconstruction to global, multi-layer settings, handling high percentages of missing data while maintaining accuracy, spatial resolution, and generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates strong novelty in applying guided diffusion models to the specific problem of global, multi-layer sea temperature reconstruction with sparse data. Existing ML-based approaches are limited to surface-level SST or local regions with denser data. ReconMOST directly addresses this gap by introducing a data-driven framework that leverages physically consistent priors learned from numerical simulations, thus enabling reconstruction in regions with minimal observations. Combining diffusion models with sparse observational guidance represents a novel approach to tackling this challenging problem.

*   **Significance:** The accurate reconstruction of ocean temperature is crucial for climate modeling, understanding global warming, predicting extreme weather events, and researching marine meteorology. The paper provides a practical solution to reconstruct sea temperature with high accuracy even with sparse real-world data (matching buoy density). By offering a robust tool for reconstructing these fields, ReconMOST can potentially contribute significantly to improved climate predictions and oceanographic research. The results achieve a substantial MSE reduction compared to not using any observations and soft extension strategy, demonstrating tangible improvements.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem and its limitations in existing research.
    *   The proposed method is well-motivated with physical interpretations for the design choices.
    *   The framework utilizes both data-driven learning (diffusion models) and physical priors (numerical simulation data) for robust reconstruction.
    *   The experimental evaluation includes ablation studies and generalization tests (CMIP6 and EN4 analysis), demonstrating the effectiveness of each component and the model's applicability to real-world datasets.
    *   Visualization results are strong and clearly demonstrate the advantages of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could delve deeper into the computational cost and efficiency of the method, especially for large-scale applications. Comparisons with classic approaches such as Kriging is missing, but would be useful.
    *   The explanation for the UNet better results over vanilla U-Net could be enhanced.
    *   The limitations of the underlying numerical models used for pre-training should be addressed more explicitly. Biases in those models could propagate to the reconstructed fields.
    *   Temporal evolution of sea temperature is not considered in this work, which would be an important extension.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a new direction for ocean reconstruction research, demonstrating the potential of guided diffusion models to combine data-driven and physics-based approaches. The open-source code availability should further encourage adoption and experimentation within the community. The methodology could be extended to other oceanic variables (e.g., salinity, ocean currents) and has promising applications in diverse observation scenarios.

* **Justification of score**: While the paper addresses an important problem and offers a novel approach with strong empirical results, there's some lack of depth into the inner workings of the algorithms that are compared, limiting a higher score. Also, the lack of a more thorough discussion of the computational costs and limitations with numerical models detracts somewhat from the overall impact. However, the clear and well-structured presentation, along with the substantial quantitative improvements, contributes to a high overall score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Pisces: An Auto-regressive Foundation Model for Image Understanding and Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10395v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyang Xu, Jiuhai Chen, Zhaojiang Lin, Xichen Pan, Lifu Huang, Tianyi Zhou, Madian Khabsa, Qifan Wang, Di Jin, Michihiro Yasunaga, Lili Yu, Xi Victoria Lin, Shaoliang Nie
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled multimodal foundation models to tackle both image understanding and generation within a unified framework. Despite these gains, unified models often underperform compared to specialized models in either task. A key challenge in developing unified models lies in the inherent differences between the visual features needed for image understanding versus generation, as well as the distinct training processes required for each modality. In this work, we introduce Pisces, an auto-regressive multimodal foundation model that addresses this challenge through a novel decoupled visual encoding architecture and tailored training techniques optimized for multimodal generation. Combined with meticulous data curation, pretraining, and finetuning, Pisces achieves competitive performance in both image understanding and image generation. We evaluate Pisces on over 20 public benchmarks for image understanding, where it demonstrates strong performance across a wide range of tasks. Additionally, on GenEval, a widely adopted benchmark for image generation, Pisces exhibits robust generative capabilities. Our extensive analysis reveals the synergistic relationship between image understanding and generation, and the benefits of using separate visual encoders, advancing the field of unified multimodal models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Pisces: An Auto-regressive Foundation Model for Image Understanding and Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PISCES, an auto-regressive multimodal foundation model designed for both image understanding and generation.  It addresses the performance gap between unified models and specialized models by using a decoupled visual encoding architecture. This architecture uses separate encoders, projection layers, and visual vector lengths optimized for each task (understanding vs. generation).  The model is trained in three stages: (1) pretraining on image-caption pairs, (2) fine-grained pretraining with detailed captions, and (3) instruction tuning.  The authors demonstrate competitive performance on over 20 image understanding benchmarks and the GenEval image generation benchmark. They also explore the synergistic relationship between image understanding and generation and show the benefits of decoupled visual encoders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the decoupled visual encoding architecture.  While other multimodal models exist, the explicit separation of encoders and pathways for understanding and generation is a distinctive design choice.  The three-stage training process is also notable, though it builds upon existing pretraining and instruction tuning techniques. The contribution is more about the *specific architecture and its impact on performance* rather than revolutionary concepts.  The idea of separate pathways is not entirely new, but its implementation and evaluation in this context demonstrate a clear benefit.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its ability to bridge the performance gap between unified and specialized models in image understanding and generation. The results on a wide range of benchmarks, surpassing even larger models like Seed-X and Chameleon in some cases, are compelling.  The analysis of the synergistic relationship between the two tasks provides valuable insights for future research.  The ablation studies demonstrating the effectiveness of the decoupled encoders further strengthens the paper's contribution. The work provides a strong case for specialized pathways within unified multimodal models, potentially influencing future architectural designs in this field.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The model performs well across a variety of tasks and benchmarks, demonstrating its effectiveness.
*   **Clear Architecture Design:** The decoupled encoding architecture is well-motivated and clearly explained.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide evidence for the benefits of the key design choices.
*   **Synergistic Relationship Analysis:** The investigation into the interaction between image understanding and generation provides valuable insights.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Evaluated on a wide range of tasks and datasets, allowing for meaningful performance comparisons.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Training Techniques:** While the three-stage training is effective, it relies on existing pretraining and instruction tuning methodologies, diminishing its novelty.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** The gains, while significant, might be viewed as incremental improvements on top of existing models rather than a disruptive breakthrough. It essentially tackles the problem by using a more specialized architecture, which is more of an *engineering* rather than a *conceptual* innovation.
*   **Dependence on Pretrained Models:** The model's performance heavily relies on the quality of pretrained LLMs and CLIP encoders, limiting its independent contribution.
*   **Lack of Detailed Analysis on Computational Cost:** Although briefly mentioned, a more detailed analysis of computational efficiency compared to specialized models would strengthen the paper.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of multimodal foundation models. The decoupled visual encoding architecture is a well-designed and effective approach for improving performance on both image understanding and generation. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their combination and the thorough evaluation make this a significant contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Time To Impeach LLM-as-a-Judge: Programs are the Future of Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10403v1)
- **Authors**: Tzu-Heng Huang, Harit Vishwakarma, Frederic Sala
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are widely used to evaluate the quality of LLM generations and responses, but this leads to significant challenges: high API costs, uncertain reliability, inflexible pipelines, and inherent biases. To address these, we introduce PAJAMA (Program-As-a-Judge for Automated Model Assessment), a new alternative that uses LLMs to synthesize executable judging programs instead of directly scoring responses. These synthesized programs can be stored and run locally, costing orders of magnitude less while providing interpretable, and auditable judging logic that can be easily adapted. Program-based judges mitigate biases, improving judgment consistency by 15.83% and reducing biased responses by 23.7% on average compared to a Qwen2.5-14B-based LLM-as-a-judge. When program judgments are distilled into a model, PAJAMA outperforms LLM-as-a-judge on the challenging CHAT-HARD subset of RewardBench, outperforming metrics by 2.19% on Prometheus and 8.67% on the JudgeLM dataset, all at three orders of magnitude lower cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Time To Impeach LLM-as-a-Judge: Programs are the Future of Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PAJAMA (Program-As-a-Judge for Automated Model Assessment), a novel approach to evaluating LLM generations.  Instead of using LLMs directly to score responses (LLM-as-a-judge), PAJAMA uses LLMs to *synthesize executable judging programs*. These programs, written in code (e.g., Python), encode evaluation criteria and are then executed locally to score responses. This aims to address the high cost, uncertain reliability, inflexibility, and inherent biases associated with traditional LLM-as-a-judge systems.  The synthesized programs are combined using weak supervision techniques to create an ensemble of judges. Experiments show PAJAMA achieves competitive performance with LLM-as-a-judge systems at a much lower cost, reduces biases, and improves consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of synthesizing *executable judging programs* instead of directly using LLMs for scoring is novel and potentially disruptive. It shifts the cost from repeated LLM inference on large datasets to a one-time synthesis of judging logic. The combination of program synthesis with weak supervision is also a noteworthy methodological contribution. However, the reliance on GPT-4o for synthesizing judging program means the idea is more of an application of existing LLMs (GPT-4o) rather than a fundamental new technology. The criteria for judging are all based on existing LLM technologies, like BERT-based analysis.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses critical limitations of the LLM-as-a-judge paradigm. The promise of reduced costs, increased transparency, and bias mitigation is significant, especially given the widespread use of LLM-based evaluation in model development and dataset curation. The results on bias reduction and improved consistency are particularly impactful.  The approach also enables interpretability since the judgement logic is transparent and auditable. The dramatic cost reduction (orders of magnitude) alone makes this potentially significant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Cost Reduction:** The empirical demonstration of significant cost savings is compelling.
    *   **Bias Mitigation:**  The experiments showing reduced bias compared to LLM-as-a-judge are strong and address a known weakness.
    *   **Interpretability and Auditability:** Turning black-box LLM judgments into transparent code allows for closer scrutiny, rubric refinement, and targeted bias correction.
    *   **Flexibility:** Ability to create ensembles of judges with different criteria is a key advantage.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper uses several standard datasets (Prometheus, JudgeLM, PandaLM, RewardBench) providing credible empirical evidence.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on a Powerful LLM for Synthesis:** The approach relies heavily on the ability of LLMs (e.g., GPT-4o) to generate effective judging programs. The quality of these programs will directly affect the performance of PAJAMA. Any issues within the synthesis LLM (like hallucination) will transfer to the judges.
    *   **Complexity of Weak Supervision:**  While weak supervision helps aggregate judgments, it introduces its own complexity in terms of labeling functions and weighting strategies.  It can be difficult to tune.
    *   **Generalizability of Programs:** The synthesized programs, even with diverse criteria, might not generalize as well as a fine-tuned LLM judge, especially to unseen evaluation tasks or different domains.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation Criteria:** The six evaluation criteria (Structure, Relevance, Readability, Bias, Factuality, Safety) are a good start, but they might not cover all relevant aspects of response quality, particularly nuanced subjective judgments.
    *   **Potential for Overfitting:** Distilling the judgment ensembles into a reward model can lead to overfitting the specific characteristics of the synthesized programs. While they try to avoid this with a hold out set, it is still possible that the result is not as general as the experiment suggests.

**Overall Assessment:**

PAJAMA presents a worthwhile and innovative approach to address key drawbacks of LLM-as-a-judge. The benefits from bias reduction and cost are significant. However, the reliance on LLMs for program synthesis adds an additional dependency on powerful LLMs, which are themselves under continuous development. The novelty of the approach is very high, but the paper falls short of creating a completely new foundational method, rather building upon existing methods in LLMs, weak supervision and program synthesis.

### PAG: Multi-Turn Reinforced LLM Self-Correction with Policy as Generative Verifier
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10406v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhua Jiang, Yuwen Xiong, Yufeng Yuan, Chao Xin, Wenyuan Xu, Yu Yue, Qianchuan Zhao, Lin Yan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in complex reasoning tasks, yet they still struggle to reliably verify the correctness of their own outputs. Existing solutions to this verification challenge often depend on separate verifier models or require multi-stage self-correction training pipelines, which limit scalability. In this paper, we propose Policy as Generative Verifier (PAG), a simple and effective framework that empowers LLMs to self-correct by alternating between policy and verifier roles within a unified multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) paradigm. Distinct from prior approaches that always generate a second attempt regardless of model confidence, PAG introduces a selective revision mechanism: the model revises its answer only when its own generative verification step detects an error. This verify-then-revise workflow not only alleviates model collapse but also jointly enhances both reasoning and verification abilities. Extensive experiments across diverse reasoning benchmarks highlight PAG's dual advancements: as a policy, it enhances direct generation and self-correction accuracy; as a verifier, its self-verification outperforms self-consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Policy as Generative Verifier (PAG), a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to enhance the self-correction capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). PAG enables LLMs to alternate between a policy role (generating solutions) and a generative verifier role (evaluating their solutions). Crucially, the model only revises its answer if its own verification step identifies an error, fostering selective revision. This verify-then-revise workflow is designed to prevent model collapse and improve both reasoning and verification abilities. The authors demonstrate PAG's effectiveness across several mathematical reasoning benchmarks, showing that it improves both direct generation accuracy and self-correction performance, surpassing existing self-consistency methods. It shows state of art performance in the self correction in the specific tasks the work covers, while also requiring no additional training stages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *selective revision* mechanism driven by a generative verifier. This contrasts with existing approaches like SCoRe, which always generate a second attempt regardless of confidence, or methods that rely on separate verifier models. Integrating both roles within a single, alternating RL paradigm is also a valuable contribution. The turn independent optimization strategy is also a valuable component to mitigate the model collapse. This combination of characteristics is what adds the most value to the proposed strategy.
*   **Significance:** PAG addresses a significant challenge in LLMs: the difficulty in reliably verifying their own outputs. By enhancing self-correction, it makes LLMs more dependable in critical domains like mathematical reasoning and scientific research. The reported improvements in accuracy and the prevention of model collapse are practically relevant. However, the heavy focus on *mathematical* reasoning limits the demonstrated generality of the approach; whether it transfers well to other reasoning domains remains an open question.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is relatively simple to implement and integrates well with existing instruction-tuned LLMs.
    *   The experimental results are strong, consistently outperforming baselines on diverse math benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of the selective revision mechanism and turn independent optimization.
    *   The paper addresses the problem of model collapse, a well-known issue in multi-turn RL.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on external ground-truth verifiers during *training* limits the applicability of PAG to tasks where such supervision is unavailable. This could be a significant constraint for real-world deployment in scenarios with limited or no labeled data.
    *   The heavy focus on mathematical reasoning limits the generality of conclusions. It's unclear if the benefits of PAG will translate to other reasoning tasks like commonsense reasoning, natural language inference, or code generation.
    *   While model collapse is addressed, more analysis of the *types* of errors PAG successfully corrects would add further depth.
    *   Although the method is framed as simple and streamlined, there's still complexity in tuning the RL training process, and hyperparameters are provided without much discussion.
*   **Potential Influence:** The PAG framework has the potential to influence future research on self-correction and reinforcement learning for LLMs. It could inspire new approaches that leverage generative verifiers and selective revision mechanisms. However, the limited scope of the benchmarks may hinder wider adoption. The results on self-verification and outperforming self consistency could also influence future research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective approach to self-correction in LLMs. However, its reliance on supervised training, the narrow focus on math reasoning tasks, and unexplored application for other reasoning types limits its overall impact on the field, as it has limited use without the training step. While the method has merits on the mathematical reasoning side, it is not entirely groundbreaking, as many works have covered this topic before, which makes it harder to asses. The theoretical analysis is weak. The writing quality could also be improved. For the outlined points, the paper deserves a solid score.

### Reasoning RAG via System 1 or System 2: A Survey on Reasoning Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Industry Challenges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10408v1)
- **Authors**: Jintao Liang, Gang Su, Huifeng Lin, You Wu, Rui Zhao, Ziyue Li
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) has emerged as a powerful framework to overcome the knowledge limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) by integrating external retrieval with language generation. While early RAG systems based on static pipelines have shown effectiveness in well-structured tasks, they struggle in real-world scenarios requiring complex reasoning, dynamic retrieval, and multi-modal integration. To address these challenges, the field has shifted toward Reasoning Agentic RAG, a paradigm that embeds decision-making and adaptive tool use directly into the retrieval process. In this paper, we present a comprehensive review of Reasoning Agentic RAG methods, categorizing them into two primary systems: predefined reasoning, which follows fixed modular pipelines to boost reasoning, and agentic reasoning, where the model autonomously orchestrates tool interaction during inference. We analyze representative techniques under both paradigms, covering architectural design, reasoning strategies, and tool coordination. Finally, we discuss key research challenges and propose future directions to advance the flexibility, robustness, and applicability of reasoning agentic RAG systems. Our collection of the relevant research has been organized into a https://github.com/ByebyeMonica/Reasoning-Agentic-RAG.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper, provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and assign a score with a detailed justification.

**Summary**

The paper presents a survey on Reasoning Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) methods, a paradigm that embeds decision-making and adaptive tool use directly into the retrieval process for Large Language Models (LLMs). It categorizes these methods into two primary systems: *predefined reasoning* (following fixed modular pipelines) and *agentic reasoning* (where the model autonomously orchestrates tool interaction). The paper analyzes representative techniques under both paradigms, covering architectural design, reasoning strategies, and tool coordination. The authors also discuss key research challenges and propose future directions to advance the flexibility, robustness, and applicability of reasoning agentic RAG systems. Furthermore, it maps the two categories (predefined vs agentic reasoning) onto System 1 and System 2 of cognitive science, respectively.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its comprehensive categorization and analysis of Reasoning Agentic RAG methods. While individual techniques within this area have been explored previously, the paper provides a structured framework for understanding the broader landscape. Mapping these techniques to System 1/System 2 is interesting but not fundamentally new. The novelty hinges on the structured view and categorization rather than the introduction of any completely novel concept or method.

*   **Significance:** RAG is a crucial technique for mitigating the limitations of LLMs, and Agentic RAG represents a significant evolution in this field. By providing a clear overview of different approaches and highlighting their strengths and weaknesses, the paper can help researchers and practitioners navigate this complex area more effectively. The discussion of future research directions is also valuable, as it identifies key challenges and opportunities for further advancement. The organization of relevant research into a Github repository is a plus, enhancing accessibility and promoting further exploration. It also successfully brings together distinct lines of RAG-related research under a unifying framework.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The paper provides a broad overview of the field, covering a wide range of techniques and approaches.
    *   **Clear Categorization:** The distinction between predefined and agentic reasoning is helpful for understanding the different design philosophies behind these systems.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The paper offers valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of different approaches, highlighting their trade-offs and limitations.
    *   **Well-Structured:** The paper is well-organized and easy to follow, making it accessible to both researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Future Directions:** the proposed directions offer a guide for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth:** As a survey, the paper cannot delve into the technical details of each method.
    *   **Lack of Empirical Evaluation:** The paper does not include any new empirical results or comparisons between different approaches. This is understandable for a survey but limits the practical implications.
    *   **System 1/System 2 Analogy**: while interesting, this is a rather superficial analogy and does not add significantly to the practical insights of the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by providing a common framework for understanding and comparing different Reasoning Agentic RAG methods. It could also guide the development of new techniques by highlighting key challenges and opportunities. The GitHub repository significantly enhances its accessibility and practical value. The survey is timely, as Agentic RAG is an emerging area with significant potential.

**Justification for Score**

The paper is a solid survey of an important and rapidly developing field. Its novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive categorization and analysis, rather than the introduction of entirely new concepts. Its significance stems from its ability to provide a clear overview of the landscape and guide future research. While it lacks new empirical results and the System 1/System 2 analogy has limited value, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. It is a valuable contribution that is likely to be widely read and cited. Therefore, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI

### Burn After Reading: Do Multimodal Large Language Models Truly Capture Order of Events in Image Sequences?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10415v1)
- **Authors**: Yingjin Song, Yupei Du, Denis Paperno, Albert Gatt
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces the TempVS benchmark, which focuses on temporal grounding and reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in image sequences. TempVS consists of three main tests (i.e., event relation inference, sentence ordering and image ordering), each accompanied with a basic grounding test. TempVS requires MLLMs to rely on both visual and linguistic modalities to understand the temporal order of events. We evaluate 38 state-of-the-art MLLMs, demonstrating that models struggle to solve TempVS, with a substantial performance gap compared to human capabilities. We also provide fine-grained insights that suggest promising directions for future research. Our TempVS benchmark data and code are available at https://github.com/yjsong22/TempVS.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**
The paper introduces TempVS, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the temporal grounding and reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) when processing image sequences. TempVS consists of three main tests: event relation inference, sentence ordering, and image ordering, each accompanied by a basic grounding test. The benchmark utilizes visual stories, making it hard for models to bypass considering both linguistic and visual modalities. The authors evaluate 38 state-of-the-art MLLMs, finding that models struggle on TempVS. The paper also includes a fine-grained analysis of the results, exploring the impact of linguistic structure, event distance, and chain-of-thought prompting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the specific focus on *temporal reasoning* in *multi-event* image sequences, which hasn't been comprehensively addressed by existing benchmarks. Most existing benchmarks primarily focus on single image tasks or cross-image recognition without delving deeply into temporal dynamics. While some benchmarks incorporate multi-image settings, they often center on recognition or reference rather than the understanding of how events unfold over time. TempVS therefore fills a clear gap in the evaluation landscape for MLLMs. However, sentence and image ordering tasks are not new.

*   **Significance:** TempVS has the potential to be significant for the MLLM research community by highlighting a critical area where models still struggle: understanding and reasoning about time. By providing a targeted benchmark, the paper encourages future research directions aimed at improving temporal comprehension in MLLMs. The comprehensive evaluation of many models offers a valuable starting point for comparative studies. The inclusion of a grounding task alongside the core tests is also a positive aspect, as it allows for a better understanding of where the models are failing (e.g., basic grounding vs. temporal reasoning).

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Targeted focus:** The benchmark is specifically designed to assess a challenging and under-explored area in MLLM capabilities.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes an evaluation of a broad range of MLLMs.
    *   **Detailed analysis:** The fine-grained analysis provides valuable insights into the factors that influence performance on the benchmark.
    *   **Publicly available resource:** The dataset and code are made publicly available, increasing its potential impact and usability for the research community.
    *   The benchmarks explicitly removes the ability of models to use shortcuts such as by focusing on single images or frames, or using commonsense reasoning alone.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Task selection:** The selected tasks, while relevant, are fairly standard (ordering and inference). While the multi-event nature makes it more challenging, the basic format is quite common. More creative and novel tasks would have boosted the benchmark's novelty and impact.
    *   **Data diversity:** While the benchmark includes different sources, further increasing the diversity of the image sequences (e.g., more diverse scenarios, real-world videos) would make the benchmark more robust.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While the evaluation uses standard accuracy metrics, additional or more nuanced metrics might reveal finer-grained differences in model performance.
    *   Reliance on multiple-choice format (which is a weakness, however, this limitation is acknowledged in the "limitations" section)

*   **Potential Influence:** TempVS could spur research into new architectural designs, training objectives, and/or post-training methods to specifically enhance temporal reasoning in MLLMs. It could also be used to track progress in this area over time, as models improve. The dataset could also be extended or adapted for related tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

TempVS represents a valuable contribution to the field by addressing the need for targeted evaluation of temporal reasoning capabilities in MLLMs. While the tasks themselves are not entirely new, the focus on multi-event image sequences and the comprehensive evaluation provide a solid foundation for future research. The limitations regarding task creativity and data diversity temper the overall impact. However, the publicly available resource and the fine-grained analysis make this a useful and significant contribution to the community, justifying a score of 7. It's a solid, well-executed benchmark, but there's room for further innovation in the tasks themselves and in the breadth of the data included.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Sound Replace Vision in LLaVA With Token Substitution?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10416v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Vosoughi, Jing Bi, Pinxin Liu, Yunlong Tang, Chenliang Xu
- **Abstract**: While multimodal systems have achieved impressive advances, they typically rely on text-aligned representations rather than directly integrating audio and visual inputs. This reliance can limit the use of acoustic information in tasks requiring nuanced audio understanding. In response, SoundCLIP explores direct audio-visual integration within multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by substituting CLIP's visual tokens with audio representations and selecting sound-relevant patch tokens in models such as LLaVA. We investigate two configurations: (1) projecting audio features into CLIP's visual manifold via a multilayer perceptron trained with InfoNCE on paired audio-video segments, and (2) preserving raw audio embeddings with minimal dimensional adjustments. Experiments with five state-of-the-art audio encoders reveal a fundamental trade-off. While audio-to-video retrieval performance increases dramatically (up to 44 percentage points in Top-1 accuracy) when audio is projected into CLIP's space, text generation quality declines. Encoders pre-trained with text supervision (CLAP, Whisper, ImageBind) maintain stronger generative capabilities than those focused primarily on audiovisual alignment (Wav2CLIP, AudioCLIP), highlighting the value of language exposure for generation tasks. We introduce WhisperCLIP, an architecture that fuses intermediate representations from Whisper, as well as AudioVisual Event Evaluation (AVE-2), a dataset of 580,147 three-second audiovisual clips with fine-grained alignment annotations. Our findings challenge the assumption that stronger cross-modal alignment necessarily benefits all multimodal tasks; instead, a Pareto frontier emerges wherein optimal performance depends on balancing retrieval accuracy with text generation quality. Codes and datasets: https://github.com/ali-vosoughi/SoundCLIP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Can Sound Replace Vision in LLaVA With Token Substitution?":

**Summary:**

The paper explores direct audio-visual integration in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), specifically LLaVA, by substituting CLIP's visual tokens with audio representations. Instead of relying on text as an intermediary, SoundCLIP framework directly aligns audio and visual features. The authors investigate two configurations: (1) projecting audio features into CLIP's visual manifold using a multi-layer perceptron (MLP), and (2) preserving raw audio embeddings with minimal adjustments. Experiments with various audio encoders reveal a fundamental trade-off: stronger alignment with CLIP's visual space significantly improves audio-to-video retrieval, but reduces text generation quality. The authors introduce WhisperCLIP, an architecture that fuses intermediate Whisper representations, and the AVE-2 dataset, a new audiovisual dataset with detailed alignment annotations. The study challenges the assumption that tighter cross-modal alignment always benefits downstream tasks and highlights the importance of balancing retrieval accuracy with generation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its direct exploration of audio-visual token substitution, bypassing text as an intermediary in LLaVA.  This is a valid direction to explore, given the limitations of text-centric multimodal approaches.  The identification and quantification of the trade-off between retrieval and generation performance are also significant, as it contradicts the common belief that stronger cross-modal alignment is unilaterally beneficial. WhisperCLIP, while not entirely novel as layer fusion techniques exist, is a practical contribution tailored to addressing the observed trade-off within this specific framework. The AVE-2 dataset addresses a gap in existing resources by providing fine-grained alignment annotations, facilitating more detailed analysis of audio-visual interactions.
* **Significance:** The findings have implications for the design and training of future MLLMs. The identified trade-off necessitates task-specific optimization strategies, suggesting that a one-size-fits-all approach to cross-modal alignment is suboptimal. The emphasis on preserving modality-specific detail for generation tasks is valuable.  The introduction of AVE-2 and WhisperCLIP can benefit the community by providing new benchmarks and architectures for further research. However, the overall impact is somewhat constrained by the reliance on a specific architecture (LLaVA). Also, while the authors explored several encoders, it is not clear how the conclusions can be generalized to other model architectures. It would have been beneficial to show some evidence of transferability beyond the LLaVA framework.
* **Strengths:**
    * Clear articulation of the problem and motivation.
    * Thorough experimental evaluation with multiple datasets and audio encoders.
    * Identification and quantification of the retrieval-generation trade-off.
    * Introduction of practical resources (WhisperCLIP and AVE-2).
    * Good documentation in the Appendix provides greater insight and detail to the experiments.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited generalizability to other MLLM architectures beyond LLaVA.
    * Relatively incremental improvement in generation quality compared to retrieval gains.  While the authors highlight the trade-off, showing significant improvement across both metrics would be more impressive.
    * Potential overstatement of claims regarding "challenging" existing assumptions. While the trade-off is important, it's not necessarily a complete overturning of previous understandings.
    * The work relies on existing pre-trained audio encoders, which makes it hard to assess the role and value of the SoundCLIP framework.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research on audio-visual MLLMs by prompting more careful consideration of the trade-offs between alignment and modality-specific information. The AVE-2 dataset will likely become a valuable benchmark for evaluating alignment methods. The WhisperCLIP architecture offers a practical approach to integrating audio into LLaVA-like models, potentially inspiring similar fusion techniques.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution with clear experimental results and valuable resources. The identified trade-off is an important observation that moves the field forward. The weaknesses primarily relate to the limited scope of the approach within a particular architecture and the incremental nature of the improvements in certain areas.
The introduction of WhisperCLIP and AVE-2 would also benefit future research and hence strengthens this paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond the Battlefield: Framing Analysis of Media Coverage in Conflict Reporting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10421v1)
- **Authors**: Avneet Kaur, Arnav Arora
- **Abstract**: Framing used by news media, especially in times of conflict, can have substantial impact on readers' opinion, potentially aggravating the conflict itself. Current studies on the topic of conflict framing have limited insights due to their qualitative nature or only look at surface level generic frames without going deeper. In this work, we identify indicators of war and peace journalism, as outlined by prior work in conflict studies, in a corpus of news articles reporting on the Israel-Palestine war. For our analysis, we use computational approaches, using a combination of frame semantics and large language models to identify both communicative framing and its connection to linguistic framing. Our analysis reveals a higher focus on war based reporting rather than peace based. We also show substantial differences in reporting across the US, UK, and Middle Eastern news outlets in framing who the assailant and victims of the conflict are, surfacing biases within the media.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates framing biases in news media coverage of the Israel-Palestine conflict using NLP techniques to operationalize Galtung's framework of war vs. peace journalism. It analyzes a corpus of news articles from the US, UK, and Middle East, extracting generic, issue-specific (war/peace journalism indicators), and semantic frames. The study reveals a higher focus on war-based reporting across all regions, identifies differences in framing assailant/victim roles (Hamas more often the assailant in Western media, the opposite in Middle Eastern media, for example), and highlights the prevalence of demonizing language. The paper combines frame semantics with LLMs to uncover communicative and linguistic framing biases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its **computational** application of Galtung's established (but typically qualitatively applied) framework to a large-scale analysis of conflict framing. Prior works tend to be qualitative or surface-level quantitative. The combination of generic, issue-specific, and semantic framing analysis provides a multi-faceted view. The operationalization of Galtung's categories into NLP tasks is another novel contribution. It leverages modern NLP techniques to scale and automate analysis previously confined to manual methods.
*   **Significance:** The research is significant because media framing plays a crucial role in shaping public opinion, especially in volatile conflict situations. Identifying and quantifying biases helps to increase media accountability and promote more balanced reporting. The analysis can also inform media literacy programs and highlight potential areas of polarization.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear Research Questions: The paper clearly defines its goals and objectives.
    *   Well-Defined Methodology: The methodology is well-defined and replicable, combining LLMs, semantic frame parsing, and topic modeling.
    *   Interesting Findings: The results highlight quantifiable differences in framing across regions, indicating significant biases in media reporting.
    *   Robust Dataset: The paper has a substantial dataset with 22k articles, which makes the analysis more credible.
    *   Integration of Theory and Method: It successfully integrates the social science framework of Galtung with modern computational techniques.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited LLM Application: Due to computational limitations, LLM based issue-specific framing was done on a smaller subset of articles.
    *   English-Only Sources: The use of only English-language sources limits the scope of the analysis and may miss important regional nuances.
    *   Bias of NLP Models: Acknowledges the possibility of biases in the LLMs and frame semantic parsers used.
    *   Qualitative Analysis of LLM Outputs: Whilst LLM outputs are grounded with extraction, some level of manual qualitative analysis is needed to validate LLM outputs. This can introduce subjectivity, although this is mitigated by using grounded LLM prompting that encourages the model to extract explicit evidence.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on conflict framing and media bias. It also has practical implications for media literacy, journalism ethics, and peace studies. It provides a framework that can be applied to other conflicts and media ecosystems to understand the nuanced ways in which news is shaped.
* **Justification of Score**:

Given the novelty in combining an established social science framework with modern NLP techniques for large scale conflict framing analysis, the significance of the topic in a world saturated with media bias, and the clear and repeatable methodology, the paper warrants a score above average. The weaknesses, namely the limitations of applying the LLM extraction to a sample subset and the English-only source limitations detract from its score, however the research provides substantial quantitative evidence to support its claims.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PAL: Probing Audio Encoders via LLMs -- A Study of Information Transfer from Audio Encoders to LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10423v1)
- **Authors**: Tony Alex, Wish Suharitdamrong, Sara Atito, Armin Mustafa, Philip J. B. Jackson, Imran Razzak, Muhammad Awais
- **Abstract**: The integration of audio perception capabilities into Large Language Models (LLMs) has enabled significant advances in Audio-LLMs. Although application-focused developments, particularly in curating training data for specific capabilities e.g., audio reasoning, have progressed rapidly, the underlying mechanisms that govern efficient transfer of rich semantic representations from audio encoders to LLMs remain under-explored. We conceptualize effective audio-LLM interaction as the LLM's ability to proficiently probe the audio encoder representations to satisfy textual queries. This paper presents a systematic investigation on how architectural design choices can affect that. Beginning with a standard Pengi/LLaVA-style audio-LLM architecture, we propose and evaluate several modifications guided by hypotheses derived from mechanistic interpretability studies and LLM operational principles. Our experiments demonstrate that: (1) delaying audio integration until the LLM's initial layers establish textual context that enhances its ability to probe the audio representations for relevant information; (2) the LLM can proficiently probe audio representations exclusively through LLM layer's attention submodule, without requiring propagation to its Feed-Forward Network (FFN) submodule; (3) an efficiently integrated ensemble of diverse audio encoders provides richer, complementary representations, thereby broadening the LLM's capacity to probe a wider spectrum of audio information. All hypotheses are evaluated using an identical three-stage training curriculum on a dataset of 5.6 million audio-text pairs, ensuring controlled comparisons.
