*   **Practical Relevance:** The inclusion of real-world applications, datasets, and evaluation protocols adds practical value for participants.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Deeper Technical Details:**  Being a tutorial outline, the paper doesn't delve into the technical intricacies of specific LLM-based techniques. This is expected, but the success of the tutorial depends on effectively conveying these concepts at an appropriate level of detail.
*   **Potential for Overlap:** There is the potential for significant overlap with existing literature on general LLM-based dialogue systems if the tutorial isn't sufficiently focused on the *search* aspect.
*   **Limited Discussion on Challenges:**  While the tutorial mentions opportunities and challenges, more emphasis on the open problems and limitations of LLMs in conversational search (e.g., hallucination, bias, cost) would strengthen the contribution.
*   **Future Impact:** Given the rate of LLM developments, it will be important to continue to update such a tutorial. This is not a weakness *per se*, but it's a real consideration.

**Justification for Score:**

The tutorial's strength lies in its *timeliness* and its specific focus on the interplay between established conversational search techniques and the disruptive influence of LLMs. This offers a practical guide and overview that is crucial for a wide audience. However, the lack of technical depth and the potential for overlap with more general LLM tutorials limit its overall impact on the research community. It is not a research paper and lacks the detailed empirical support that would raise it higher in the scale.

The score aims to reflect the value of the work as a tutorial synthesizing existing and current literature in an active area of research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GigaVideo-1: Advancing Video Generation via Automatic Feedback with 4 GPU-Hours Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10639v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyi Bao, Jindi Lv, Xiaofeng Wang, Zheng Zhu, Xinze Chen, YuKun Zhou, Jiancheng Lv, Xingang Wang, Guan Huang
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in diffusion models has greatly enhanced video generation quality, yet these models still require fine-tuning to improve specific dimensions like instance preservation, motion rationality, composition, and physical plausibility. Existing fine-tuning approaches often rely on human annotations and large-scale computational resources, limiting their practicality. In this work, we propose GigaVideo-1, an efficient fine-tuning framework that advances video generation without additional human supervision. Rather than injecting large volumes of high-quality data from external sources, GigaVideo-1 unlocks the latent potential of pre-trained video diffusion models through automatic feedback. Specifically, we focus on two key aspects of the fine-tuning process: data and optimization. To improve fine-tuning data, we design a prompt-driven data engine that constructs diverse, weakness-oriented training samples. On the optimization side, we introduce a reward-guided training strategy, which adaptively weights samples using feedback from pre-trained vision-language models with a realism constraint. We evaluate GigaVideo-1 on the VBench-2.0 benchmark using Wan2.1 as the baseline across 17 evaluation dimensions. Experiments show that GigaVideo-1 consistently improves performance on almost all the dimensions with an average gain of about 4% using only 4 GPU-hours. Requiring no manual annotations and minimal real data, GigaVideo-1 demonstrates both effectiveness and efficiency. Code, model, and data will be publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GigaVideo-1, a lightweight fine-tuning framework designed to improve the quality of text-to-video (T2V) generation models without relying on human supervision or large-scale datasets. The framework consists of two main components: a prompt-driven data engine that generates diverse and targeted training samples based on identified weaknesses of the pre-trained model, and a reward-guided training strategy that uses feedback from frozen vision-language models (VLM) to adaptively weight training samples and maintain alignment with real-world data distributions. The authors demonstrate that GigaVideo-1 can significantly enhance the performance of T2V models on various dimensions with limited computation and data resources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to fine-tuning T2V models. The combination of a prompt-driven data engine and a reward-guided training strategy is a unique approach to addressing the limitations of existing fine-tuning methods. Specifically, the automatic generation of weakness-oriented data bypasses the data bottleneck, a significant advantage. The use of frozen vision-language models for automatic feedback further reduces the reliance on human annotation and expensive reward model training. However, the idea of automatic data generation and using pre-trained models for feedback is not entirely new, so it's more of an advancement and clever combination of existing concepts rather than a groundbreaking revelation.

**Significance:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of T2V generation. By reducing the reliance on human annotation and large-scale datasets, GigaVideo-1 makes high-quality fine-tuning more accessible to researchers with limited resources. The demonstrated efficiency of the framework (only 4 GPU-hours) is particularly impressive.  The results on VBench-2.0 demonstrate that the proposed framework significantly improves the performance of T2V models on multiple dimensions and provides a generalizable performance boost compared to the base model. The idea of addressing known weaknesses of the underlying model is useful in practice. The ablation studies provide insight into the contribution of each component.

**Strengths:**

*   **Data Efficiency:** The framework demonstrates the ability to achieve significant performance improvements with minimal data and computational resources, a major advantage over existing approaches.
*   **Automatic Feedback:** The use of frozen VLMs for automatic feedback eliminates the need for human annotation, making the fine-tuning process more scalable and efficient.
*   **Targeted Data Generation:** The prompt-driven data engine allows for the generation of training samples that are specifically tailored to address the weaknesses of the pre-trained model.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors evaluate the framework on a wide range of dimensions using the VBench-2.0 benchmark, demonstrating its generalizability and effectiveness.
*   **Clear and Well-Written Paper:** The paper is well-organized and clearly written, making it easy to understand the proposed approach and its contributions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on VLM Quality:** The performance of the reward-guided training strategy relies on the quality and accuracy of the pre-trained vision-language models used for feedback. Errors or biases in the VLMs could negatively impact the fine-tuning process. If the feedback system has biases or gaps then these may well be perpetuated.
*   **Limited Coverage:** The paper mentions that limited number of training samples are labeled along certain dimensions due to CoTracker2 strict definition. Expanding prompt pool in future is mentioned, but it is still a limitation in the current implementation.
*   **Potential for Overfitting:**  Although mitigated by the realism constraint, there's still a risk of overfitting to the synthetic data distribution, especially if the prompt-driven data engine generates biased or unrealistic samples.
*   **Generalizability of Weaknesses:** The identification of "weaknesses" is somewhat model-specific.  The prompts generated to target these weaknesses might not be as effective with different base T2V architectures.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field. While the approach builds on existing techniques, the way in which the authors combine them and implement the system results in a practical and efficient fine-tuning strategy that can be adopted by researchers and practitioners with limited resources.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a solid advancement in the T2V field. The work balances novelty and practical impact well. The significant efficiency gains and reliance on existing models for feedback are notable strengths. However, the work is not completely groundbreaking as it builds upon previous methods and exhibits some weaknesses in its dependency on VLM accuracy and model-specific weakness identification.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Spelling-out is not Straightforward: LLMs' Capability of Tokenization from Token to Characters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10641v1)
- **Authors**: Tatsuya Hiraoka, Kentaro Inui
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) can spell out tokens character by character with high accuracy, yet they struggle with more complex character-level tasks, such as identifying compositional subcomponents within tokens. In this work, we investigate how LLMs internally represent and utilize character-level information during the spelling-out process. Our analysis reveals that, although spelling out is a simple task for humans, it is not handled in a straightforward manner by LLMs. Specifically, we show that the embedding layer does not fully encode character-level information, particularly beyond the first character. As a result, LLMs rely on intermediate and higher Transformer layers to reconstruct character-level knowledge, where we observe a distinct "breakthrough" in their spelling behavior. We validate this mechanism through three complementary analyses: probing classifiers, identification of knowledge neurons, and inspection of attention weights.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates how Large Language Models (LLMs) internally process and represent character-level information when spelling out tokens. The authors identify a paradox: LLMs are proficient at spelling out entire tokens character by character but struggle with more granular character-level manipulations (e.g., identifying a specific character at a fixed position). The research reveals that token embeddings do not fully encode character-level information. Instead, LLMs rely on subsequent Transformer layers to reconstruct this information, observing a "breakthrough" point where character identities become reliably detectable. The authors use probing classifiers, neuron analysis, and attention weight inspection to validate their findings, demonstrating that the locus of character knowledge aligns with the breakthrough point.  They conclude that spelling out is a learned task relying on identifying the target token and retrieving character-level information rather than simple information extraction from token embeddings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel investigation into a specific aspect of LLM behavior: how they handle character-level information during spelling out.  While previous work has noted LLMs' limitations in fine-grained character manipulation, this paper delves deeper into *where* and *how* LLMs manage to spell out tokens despite these limitations.  The identification of the "breakthrough" layer and its validation through multiple analytical methods contributes meaningfully to the existing body of knowledge. However, the methodology used (probing, attention analysis, neuron ablation) are fairly standard interpretability techniques, limiting the methodological novelty.

**Significance:** Understanding the internal workings of LLMs, especially regarding character-level information, is crucial for improving their reliability in tasks requiring such understanding, such as morphological inflection, typo correction, and handling non-standard spellings. The findings have implications for developing more robust and character-aware LLMs. If LLMs indeed perform this task via retrieval then a more direct compositional architecture or direct embedding could improve efficiency and performance across tasks. However, the results may only be applicable to relatively simple spelling-out tasks and to the specific architecture of the evaluated models. Further validation on other LLMs, especially those with distinct tokenization strategies, is needed.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly Defined Research Question:** The paper addresses a well-defined paradox and provides a clear line of inquiry.
*   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The use of multiple analytical methods (probing, neuron analysis, attention weights) strengthens the validity of the findings.
*   **Well-Structured and Articulated:** The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the methodology and results.
*   **Relevance to the Field:** The research is relevant to the broader goals of understanding and improving LLM capabilities.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to spelling-out tasks and lowercase alphabet tokens. Generalizability to other tasks, languages, or character sets might be limited.
*   **Methodological Novelty:** The methods employed (probing, attention analysis) are well-established, limiting the overall novelty.
*   **Reliance on Interpretability Methods:** The conclusions rely heavily on the interpretation of probing classifiers, attention weights, and neuron activity, which are themselves subjects of ongoing research and debate. The results do not necessarily tell the *whole* story of *how* the models are reasoning.
*   **Model Variance:** While trends are observed across models, the divergent behavior of Amber-6.7B highlights the potential for model-specific idiosyncrasies, limiting the generalizability of the results.
*   **Lack of Actionable Recommendations:** The paper doesn't provide concrete suggestions for *how* to improve the character-level capabilities of LLMs based on their findings. Although, there are broader implications about the character awareness of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a useful investigation into the internal workings of LLMs during a relatively simple, yet revealing, task. The findings contribute to our understanding of how LLMs represent and process character-level information. However, limitations in scope, methodological novelty, and a lack of concrete recommendations temper its overall impact. The dependence on common interpretability techniques, which may or may not fully capture the inner workings of the model, also limits the overall significance of the results.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Data Shifts Hurt CoT: A Theoretical Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10647v1)
- **Authors**: Lang Yin, Debangshu Banerjee, Gagandeep Singh
- **Abstract**: Chain of Thought (CoT) has been applied to various large language models (LLMs) and proven to be effective in improving the quality of outputs. In recent studies, transformers are proven to have absolute upper bounds in terms of expressive power, and consequently, they cannot solve many computationally difficult problems. However, empowered by CoT, transformers are proven to be able to solve some difficult problems effectively, such as the $k$-parity problem. Nevertheless, those works rely on two imperative assumptions: (1) identical training and testing distribution, and (2) corruption-free training data with correct reasoning steps. However, in the real world, these assumptions do not always hold. Although the risks of data shifts have caught attention, our work is the first to rigorously study the exact harm caused by such shifts to the best of our knowledge. Focusing on the $k$-parity problem, in this work we investigate the joint impact of two types of data shifts: the distribution shifts and data poisoning, on the quality of trained models obtained by a well-established CoT decomposition. In addition to revealing a surprising phenomenon that CoT leads to worse performance on learning parity than directly generating the prediction, our technical results also give a rigorous and comprehensive explanation of the mechanistic reasons of such impact.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Data Shifts Hurt CoT: A Theoretical Study":

**Summary:**

The paper theoretically investigates the impact of data shifts on Chain of Thought (CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs), specifically focusing on the k-parity problem. The authors examine two types of data shifts: distribution shifts (where the training and testing data distributions differ) and data poisoning (where training data contains incorrect reasoning steps). They develop a theoretical framework to characterize the necessary and sufficient conditions for CoT to succeed under these shifts.  A key finding is that CoT can be surprisingly vulnerable to even small amounts of data poisoning, and that non-uniform input distributions, which intuitively should *help* the learning process by "leaking" information about relevant bits, can actually degrade CoT performance. The paper offers a detailed analysis of the gradient updates and attention mechanisms involved, providing a mechanistic explanation for these effects.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its *rigorous theoretical treatment* of the impact of data shifts on CoT reasoning. Prior work had primarily focused on CoT in idealized settings with perfect training data and identical training/testing distributions. Addressing the practical concerns of distribution shifts and data poisoning, especially in a *provable* way, is a significant step forward. The counterintuitive finding that distribution shifts can *hurt* performance is a notable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for the application of CoT in real-world scenarios. The finding that CoT is vulnerable to data poisoning highlights the need for robust data cleaning and validation procedures when training CoT-enabled LLMs. The analysis of distribution shifts can guide the design of training strategies that are more resilient to variations in input data. The theoretical framework provides valuable insights into the inner workings of CoT and can inform the development of more reliable CoT methods. The k-parity is a limited domain, however, real work applications for CoT are more nuanced.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a well-defined and rigorous theoretical framework.
    *   The analysis is detailed and comprehensive, providing a mechanistic understanding of the effects of data shifts.
    *   The findings are counterintuitive and have practical implications.
    *   The paper clearly identifies and explains the limitations of the analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The analysis is limited to the k-parity problem, which is a simplified setting.  The extent to which the results generalize to more complex and realistic tasks is unclear. While the problem offers analytic tractability, it comes at the expense of real-world applicability.
    *   The theoretical framework is relatively complex, which could make it difficult for practitioners to directly apply the results.
    *   The paper states that there are 35 experiments that are run, but the presentation of the results makes it difficult to ascertain whether the empirical validation truly matches the theoretical insight.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to influence future research on CoT by: (1) motivating the development of CoT methods that are more robust to data shifts; (2) encouraging the theoretical analysis of CoT in more realistic settings; and (3) providing a foundation for understanding the interplay between CoT, data quality, and generalization performance.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper presents a strong theoretical contribution to a field that, up to this point, has largely been empirical. The rigorous analysis and non-intuitive insights are valuable, however, the significant limitation of scope and difficulty in fully validating results makes this fall short of the best in the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models-Empowered Wireless Networks: Fundamentals, Architecture, and Challenges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10651v1)
- **Authors**: Latif U. Khan, Maher Guizani, Sami Muhaidat, Choong Seon Hong
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of wireless networks has resulted in numerous challenges stemming from their extensive demands for quality of service towards innovative quality of experience metrics (e.g., user-defined metrics in terms of sense of physical experience for haptics applications). In the meantime, large language models (LLMs) emerged as promising solutions for many difficult and complex applications/tasks. These lead to a notion of the integration of LLMs and wireless networks. However, this integration is challenging and needs careful attention in design. Therefore, in this article, we present a notion of rational wireless networks powered by \emph{telecom LLMs}, namely, \emph{LLM-native wireless systems}. We provide fundamentals, vision, and a case study of the distributed implementation of LLM-native wireless systems. In the case study, we propose a solution based on double deep Q-learning (DDQN) that outperforms existing DDQN solutions. Finally, we provide open challenges.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper and will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and novelty assessment with a justification score.

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of integrating large language models (LLMs) with wireless networks to create "LLM-native" wireless systems. It argues that traditional ML-enabled wireless networks have limitations in handling novel applications, insufficient training data, and scalability. The authors propose a telecom LLM agent-based architecture for enabling emerging wireless applications, including intelligent reasoning and autonomous operation. They present a case study of joint distributed LLM agents learning and task offloading, using a modified Double Deep Q-Learning (DDQN) approach combined with convex optimization for resource allocation.  Finally, the paper outlines open challenges for this emerging field.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timeliness:** The paper addresses a very relevant and emerging research area – the intersection of LLMs and wireless networks. This integration holds significant promise, given the rise of sophisticated applications like the metaverse and autonomous systems.
    *   **Clearly Defined Problem Space:** The paper identifies specific limitations of traditional ML in wireless systems, providing a strong motivation for exploring LLM-based solutions.
    *   **Comprehensive Vision:** The telecom LLM agent-based architecture offers a concrete starting point for designing intelligent wireless systems, outlining key components and functions.
    *   **Technical Contribution:** The case study, focusing on joint learning and task offloading with a modified DDQN, provides a tangible example of how to implement LLM-native wireless systems. The inclusion of convex optimization for resource allocation demonstrates practical considerations.
    *   **Discussion of Open Challenges:** The paper acknowledges and outlines the challenges in multi-model edge LLMs and resource optimization, further emphasizing the research areas to focus on.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the case study presents a modified DDQN approach, the performance evaluation is relatively limited in scope and context.  A deeper and more comprehensive evaluation with extensive simulations/real-world experiments could greatly bolster the conclusions. The results should be compared against more sophisticated baseline algorithms than simply standard DDQN.
    *   **Lack of Depth in Technical Details:** The descriptions of LLM agent architecture and interaction could be more detailed, leading to more practical insights. The paper lacks in-depth discussion about the technical challenges of deploying large models at the edge, especially regarding memory footprint and inference latency.
    *   **Incremental Novelty in the Solution:** While the proposed combination of DDQN and convex optimization is practical, it doesn't represent a groundbreaking methodological advancement.  The novelty primarily lies in applying these techniques to the specific problem of LLM-enabled wireless networks.
    *   **Limited Consideration of Ethical Implications:** The paper does not delve into the ethical considerations surrounding LLMs in wireless networks, such as privacy, bias, and potential misuse.

**Novelty and Significance Assessment:**

The paper possesses moderate novelty, primarily due to the timely application of LLMs to the field of wireless networking and the proposal of a comprehensive architecture. The technical solution (modified DDQN) is not fundamentally novel but provides a practical starting point. The significance lies in outlining a vision for future intelligent wireless networks and highlighting key challenges that need to be addressed.

The lack of in-depth technical discussion and limited empirical validation reduce the impact of the paper. A more comprehensive evaluation of the performance benefits and a more detailed architecture would enhance its contribution. A stronger focus on unique adaptation of the LLM models for the wireless context would significantly enhance its contribution.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I assign the paper a score of **6**. This score reflects the following reasoning:

*   The paper identifies a promising research area and offers a comprehensive architecture, suggesting the need for exploration.
*   The technical contribution, while practical, is somewhat incremental, relying on existing techniques.
*   The empirical evaluation is limited, hindering a complete assessment of the proposed solution. The evaluation should be done with different settings and more comparative analysis.
*   The paper lacks depth in technical details and consideration of ethical implications.

While the paper offers a valuable overview and direction for future research, it requires more thorough technical development and empirical validation to achieve a higher impact. The authors should focus on creating concrete solutions, extensively evaluating their performance, and addressing the ethical considerations of this technology.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### TeleMath: A Benchmark for Large Language Models in Telecom Mathematical Problem Solving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10674v1)
- **Authors**: Vincenzo Colle, Mohamed Sana, Nicola Piovesan, Antonio De Domenico, Fadhel Ayed, Merouane Debbah
- **Abstract**: The increasing adoption of artificial intelligence in telecommunications has raised interest in the capability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to address domain-specific, mathematically intensive tasks. Although recent advancements have improved the performance of LLMs in general mathematical reasoning, their effectiveness within specialized domains, such as signal processing, network optimization, and performance analysis, remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we introduce TeleMath, the first benchmark dataset specifically designed to evaluate LLM performance in solving mathematical problems with numerical solutions in the telecommunications domain. Comprising 500 question-answer (QnA) pairs, TeleMath covers a wide spectrum of topics in the telecommunications field. This paper outlines the proposed QnAs generation pipeline, starting from a selected seed of problems crafted by Subject Matter Experts. The evaluation of a wide range of open-source LLMs reveals that best performance on TeleMath is achieved by recent models explicitly designed for mathematical or logical reasoning. In contrast, general-purpose models, even those with a large number of parameters, often struggle with these challenges. We have released the dataset and the evaluation code to ease result reproducibility and support future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TeleMath paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TeleMath, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating the mathematical problem-solving capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the telecommunications domain. The dataset consists of 500 question-answer pairs, generated using a novel framework that leverages Subject Matter Experts (SMEs) and data augmentation techniques. The framework decomposes SME-provided problems into subproblems, generates blueprints (executable code or symbolic math formulas), and then instantiates these blueprints with new parameters to create synthetic QnA pairs.  The authors benchmark several open-source LLMs on TeleMath, demonstrating that reasoning-oriented models outperform general-purpose models on this specialized task. The dataset and evaluation code are publicly released.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in the *creation of a specialized benchmark dataset* for LLMs in telecommunications. While mathematical reasoning benchmarks exist, their application to specific domains is less common. The data generation pipeline is also a notable aspect, using SMEs as a starting point and augmenting their contributions to create a larger, more diverse dataset.
The originality in this work lies in the *domain-specific focus* and the *methodology for generating QnAs from expert-provided seeds*. The blueprint extraction and instantiation approach seem well-considered.

*   **Significance:** The Telecommunications industry is increasingly relying on AI. Evaluating LLMs' ability to solve mathematical problems within this domain is very useful. The paper shows that reasoning-oriented models are suitable than general-purpose models, that can help the community to focus on more suitable models for telecommunication applications.
The limitations acknowledged by the authors—the limited seed dataset and skewed category distribution—are important points. While 500 QnA pairs is a decent start, a larger, more representative dataset would be more impactful. The emphasis on numerical answers, while simplifying evaluation, might also limit the benchmark's scope, as symbolic manipulation is often crucial in telecom.
The benchmarking is reasonably thorough, and the results highlighting the advantage of reasoning-oriented models are interesting and potentially valuable to practitioners.
The release of the dataset and code is commendable and should encourage further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and justification for the benchmark.
    *   Well-described data generation pipeline.
    *   Thorough benchmarking of several models.
    *   Public release of the dataset and evaluation code.
    *   Novel approach for augmenting the dataset, with focus on SME-provided seeds.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited seed dataset size.
    *   Potential bias towards models used in the generation pipeline.
    *   Skewed category distribution within the dataset.
    *   Emphasis on numerical answers, potentially limiting scope.

*   **Potential Influence:** TeleMath has the potential to become a valuable resource for researchers working on applying LLMs to telecommunications. It can drive the development of more specialized and effective models for this domain. The insights gained from this benchmark can also inform the design of future datasets in other specialized fields.

*   **Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing TeleMath, a much needed domain-specific mathematical problem-solving benchmark for the telecommunications area. The data generation pipeline is well-designed and explained, and the benchmarking provides valuable insights into the performance of different LLMs on this task. While the dataset has limitations in size and category distribution, it represents a good starting point. The public release of the dataset and code further enhances its value. The score acknowledges the novelty and significance of the work while accounting for the identified weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unsourced Adversarial CAPTCHA: A Bi-Phase Adversarial CAPTCHA Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10685v1)
- **Authors**: Xia Du, Xiaoyuan Liu, Jizhe Zhou, Zheng Lin, Chi-man Pun, Zhe Chen, Wei Ni, Jun Luo
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancements in deep learning, traditional CAPTCHA schemes are increasingly vulnerable to automated attacks powered by deep neural networks (DNNs). Existing adversarial attack methods often rely on original image characteristics, resulting in distortions that hinder human interpretation and limit applicability in scenarios lacking initial input images. To address these challenges, we propose the Unsourced Adversarial CAPTCHA (UAC), a novel framework generating high-fidelity adversarial examples guided by attacker-specified text prompts. Leveraging a Large Language Model (LLM), UAC enhances CAPTCHA diversity and supports both targeted and untargeted attacks. For targeted attacks, the EDICT method optimizes dual latent variables in a diffusion model for superior image quality. In untargeted attacks, especially for black-box scenarios, we introduce bi-path unsourced adversarial CAPTCHA (BP-UAC), a two-step optimization strategy employing multimodal gradients and bi-path optimization for efficient misclassification. Experiments show BP-UAC achieves high attack success rates across diverse systems, generating natural CAPTCHAs indistinguishable to humans and DNNs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Unsourced Adversarial CAPTCHA (UAC), a novel framework for generating adversarial examples to bypass CAPTCHA systems. Unlike traditional approaches that modify existing images, UAC leverages large language models (LLMs) to create diverse and natural-looking CAPTCHAs from text prompts.  The framework has two main components: UAC for white-box attack scenarios and Bi-Path UAC (BP-UAC) for black-box attack scenarios. UAC uses EDICT for stable and high-quality adversarial example generation, while BP-UAC utilizes multi-model gradients and a bi-path optimization strategy to improve transferability in black-box settings.  Experiments show that BP-UAC achieves high attack success rates while generating CAPTCHAs that are indistinguishable from clean samples to both humans and DNNs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several key aspects:

    *   **Unsourced generation:** Moving away from modifying existing images and generating CAPTCHAs directly from text prompts using LLMs is a significant departure from traditional adversarial CAPTCHA techniques. This allows for a much larger and more diverse generation space.
    *   **Black-box adversarial generation with generative models:** Achieving near-perfect attack success rates against unknown black-box models with a generative approach is a noteworthy accomplishment. This is due to the introduction of novel bi-path optimization, the multi-model gradient averaging strategy and, most notably, an effective combination of the CLIP pre-training and EDICT frameworks.
    *   **Integration of LLMs and diffusion models for security:** While both LLMs and diffusion models are actively researched, their combined application for adversarial CAPTCHA generation is novel. The use of LLMs to ensure prompt accuracy and the subsequent use of a diffusion model guided by this prompt to generate images has not been previously explored.

*   **Significance:** The research addresses a critical challenge in cybersecurity: the increasing vulnerability of CAPTCHA systems to AI-powered attacks. The proposed framework demonstrates a new approach to generate highly effective adversarial CAPTCHAs, which can be leveraged to build defenses that can withstand future attacks.  The approach also has broader implications for adversarial robustness testing in scenarios where access to original data is limited or restricted due to copyright constraints.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive experimental evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experiments across different models (white-box and black-box), and settings (different learning rates, attack steps). These experiments validate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework, comparing its performance against state-of-the-art methods.
    *   **Detailed ablation studies:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the importance of each component of the proposed framework, especially the combination of LLM prompt guidance and the use of clip models.
    *   **Clear writing and organization:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the proposed framework and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational cost:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational cost associated with generating the adversarial CAPTCHAs. Diffusion models are known to be computationally expensive, and the integration of LLMs adds further overhead. While the paper mentions that the method is practical for real-time applications, some additional details about the inference time and resource usage would be valuable.
    *   **Potential misuse:** While adversarial CAPTCHAs are useful for defense, the techniques described in this paper could be misused for malicious purposes. The paper should at least acknowledge this limitation and briefly discuss potential mitigation strategies.
    *   **Limited defense analysis:** The study analyzes the model with different known defense models. While it's shown to perform with high ASR, further analysis could involve testing defenses explicitly designed to counter unsourced adversarial generation.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of adversarial security and CAPTCHA research. The proposed framework opens up new avenues for generating robust CAPTCHAs and for testing the robustness of AI systems in scenarios where access to original data is limited. The results show that it generates near 100% ASR even under unknown models, potentially driving a more robust defense in CAPTCHA generation.

*   **Rigor of Rationale:** The score is based on the genuine advancement in attack success rate against unknown black box models, by incorporating prompt-to-image generation with robustness frameworks. The LLM+CLIP combination is well-justified with ablation experiments. There is a clear potential impact on CAPTCHA robustness and other cases where no original images are available.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of adversarial security and CAPTCHA research. The unsourced adversarial CAPTCHA framework addresses a critical challenge and achieves remarkable attack success rates, especially in black-box settings, by incorporating the LLM+CLIP models.  However, some improvements in the aspects of computational cost analysis, misuse discussion, and a more comprehensive defense analysis would make the paper even more impactful.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Large Language Models for Detection of Life-Threatening Texts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10687v1)
- **Authors**: Thanh Thi Nguyen, Campbell Wilson, Janis Dalins
- **Abstract**: Detecting life-threatening language is essential for safeguarding individuals in distress, promoting mental health and well-being, and preventing potential harm and loss of life. This paper presents an effective approach to identifying life-threatening texts using large language models (LLMs) and compares them with traditional methods such as bag of words, word embedding, topic modeling, and Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers. We fine-tune three open-source LLMs including Gemma, Mistral, and Llama-2 using their 7B parameter variants on different datasets, which are constructed with class balance, imbalance, and extreme imbalance scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate a strong performance of LLMs against traditional methods. More specifically, Mistral and Llama-2 models are top performers in both balanced and imbalanced data scenarios while Gemma is slightly behind. We employ the upsampling technique to deal with the imbalanced data scenarios and demonstrate that while this method benefits traditional approaches, it does not have as much impact on LLMs. This study demonstrates a great potential of LLMs for real-world life-threatening language detection problems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for detecting life-threatening language in text. It compares the performance of fine-tuned open-source LLMs (Gemma, Mistral, and Llama-2) against traditional machine learning methods like TF-IDF, word embeddings (GloVe, Word2Vec), topic modeling (LDA, LSI), and BERT.  The LLMs are fine-tuned on datasets with varying class imbalances, and the impact of upsampling on both LLMs and traditional methods is analyzed. The results demonstrate that LLMs, especially Mistral and Llama-2, outperform traditional methods in most scenarios, even without extensive text preprocessing or upsampling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its direct application of recent LLMs (Gemma, Mistral, Llama-2) to the specific problem of life-threatening language detection and a thorough comparison with traditional methods across varying degrees of data imbalance.  While LLMs are increasingly being used for text classification, this paper provides a valuable benchmark for a specific, safety-critical task. The investigation of upsampling in imbalanced datasets and its effect on both LLMs and traditional techniques is also a useful contribution.

* **Significance:** Detecting life-threatening language is a crucial task with significant real-world implications for mental health support, crisis intervention, and public safety.  The paper's findings, demonstrating the superior performance of LLMs, highlight the potential for these models to improve the accuracy and efficiency of automated detection systems. This is particularly important given the challenges of data scarcity and class imbalance in this domain. The consistent use of different metrics beyond just accuracy is a strength.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper conducts a thorough experimental evaluation, comparing multiple LLMs and traditional methods across a range of datasets with varying class imbalances.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The analysis of the impact of upsampling on both LLMs and traditional methods provides valuable insights into the behavior of these techniques in imbalanced data scenarios.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the experimental setup, results, and analysis.
    * **Relevance:** Addresses a pressing real-world problem with a potential to improve safety and wellbeing.
    * **Use of appropriate metrics:** Employs several metrics, particularly F-scores and AUC, alongside accuracy to evaluate the performance of the classification methods.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty in Method:** The method used is fine-tuning which is well understood in the field and the paper does not propose any innovation in this part.
    * **Dataset Dependence:** The performance of machine learning models is heavily dependent on the characteristics of the training data. The study does not delve into the limitations of using publicly available datasets with dynamically generated hate speech, which may not fully represent the nuances of real-world life-threatening language.
    * **Limited ablation studies:** The paper provides limited insight into *why* LLMs perform better. What specific aspects of life-threatening language are captured more effectively by LLMs compared to traditional methods?  Ablation studies exploring different fine-tuning strategies or layers of the LLMs could provide deeper insights.
    * **Interpretability:** The paper does not address the interpretability of the LLMs. Understanding why an LLM classifies a text as life-threatening is crucial for building trust and ensuring responsible use of these models.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a valuable benchmark for future research in this area and encourages the adoption of LLMs for life-threatening language detection. The findings may also inform the development of more effective crisis intervention and support systems. However, careful consideration of dataset biases and ethical implications is essential.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper addresses a vital problem and provides a rigorous empirical comparison, its methodological novelty is somewhat limited. The fine-tuning approach is standard, and the datasets used, while relevant, may not fully capture the complexities of real-world life-threatening language. The limitations in ablation studies and interpretability also slightly reduce the score.

### Formalising Software Requirements using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10704v1)
- **Authors**: Arshad Beg, Diarmuid O'Donoghue, Rosemary Monahan
- **Abstract**: This paper is a brief introduction to our recently initiated project named VERIFAI: Traceability and verification of natural language requirements. The project addresses the challenges in the traceability and verification of formal specifications through providing support for the automatic generation of the formal specifications and the traceability of the requirements from the initial software design stage through the systems implementation and verification. Approaches explored in this project include Natural Language Processing, use of ontologies to describe the software system domain, reuse of existing software artefacts from similar systems (i.e. through similarity based reuse) and large language models to identify and declare the specifications as well as use of artificial intelligence to guide the process.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper is a brief introduction to the VERIFAI project, which aims to improve the traceability and verification of formal specifications. It addresses challenges in generating and verifying formal specifications automatically from natural language requirements, using Natural Language Processing (NLP), ontologies, software artefact reuse, and Large Language Models (LLMs). The paper includes an example of generating Dafny assertions using LLMs and a review of related work in the area of formal specification generation using LLMs. The authors intend to refine techniques for bridging the gap between informal descriptions and formal specifications via prompt engineering, chain-of-thought reasoning, and hybrid neuro-symbolic approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper, as presented, offers limited explicit novelty. The use of LLMs for formal specification generation is not a new idea, as the cited works demonstrate. The proposed VERIFAI project builds on existing research.  The explicit techniques the authors intend to explore, such as refinement of prompt engineering and incorporation of chain-of-thought reasoning, have also been investigated within the LLM literature, but their specific application within the formal specification domain has the potential to be impactful.

*   **Significance:** The VERIFAI project, if successful, could significantly impact software development. Automating the generation and verification of formal specifications reduces the time, expertise, and cost involved, making formal methods more accessible to industry. The reliance on large-scale software products and complex systems necessitates ensuring correctness, therefore bridging natural language and formal specification with automated techniques provides significant potential for cost saving and error reduction in safety-critical systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper correctly identifies a practical problem: the difficulty and expense of using formal methods in software development.
    *   The project leverages current advances in LLMs, which is a promising direction.
    *   The mention of using ontologies and software artefact reuse are valid techniques to potentially address some common challenges.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper is a brief overview with limited detail on specific techniques or approaches. It lacks concrete details regarding implementation or evaluation.
    *   The related work section is primarily a listing of other papers, without sufficient critical analysis of how VERIFAI will differentiate itself or improve upon existing approaches.
    *   The "Conclusions" section is essentially a restatement of intent, with no preliminary results or specific directions beyond general approaches.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to inspire or inform others about the ongoing effort in automating the generation of formal specifications. However, the lack of novelty and detailed discussion limits its influence in its current state.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the limited novelty of the general concept (LLMs for formal specs), the lack of specific technical contributions, and the reliance on future work, the paper's significance is potential rather than demonstrated.  The field is actively exploring these concepts, and this paper, in its current form, lacks the depth or specific innovation to be considered a major contribution. The intention to combine prompt engineering with reasoning could be significant if well executed.

### ConTextTab: A Semantics-Aware Tabular In-Context Learner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10707v1)
- **Authors**: Marco Spinaci, Marek Polewczyk, Maximilian Schambach, Sam Thelin
- **Abstract**: Tabular in-context learning (ICL) has recently achieved state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance on several tabular prediction tasks. Previously restricted to classification problems on small tables, recent advances such as TabPFN and TabICL have extended its use to larger datasets. While being architecturally efficient and well-adapted to tabular data structures, current table-native ICL architectures, being trained exclusively on synthetic data, do not fully leverage the rich semantics and world knowledge contained in real-world tabular data. On another end of this spectrum, tabular ICL models based on pretrained large language models such as TabuLa-8B integrate deep semantic understanding and world knowledge but are only able to make use of a small amount of context due to inherent architectural limitations. With the aim to combine the best of both these worlds, we introduce ConTextTab, integrating semantic understanding and alignment into a table-native ICL framework. By employing specialized embeddings for different data modalities and by training on large-scale real-world tabular data, our model is competitive with SOTA across a broad set of benchmarks while setting a new standard on the semantically rich CARTE benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ConTextTab, a novel tabular in-context learning (ICL) framework that aims to combine the strengths of both table-native ICL architectures (like TabPFN and TabICL) and LLM-based tabular ICL models (like TabuLa-8B).  ConTextTab leverages data type-specific embeddings (including semantic embeddings for column names and categorical values) and is trained on a large-scale real-world tabular dataset (T4).  The authors demonstrate that ConTextTab achieves competitive performance across a range of tabular prediction benchmarks and sets a new state-of-the-art on the semantically rich CARTE benchmark, especially in low-data regimes. The key novelty is the integration of semantic understanding and alignment into a table-native framework, addressing a recognized gap in existing table-native ICL approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques: a table-native ICL architecture (TabPFN) enhanced with data type-specific embeddings (including semantic embeddings for column names) and trained on real-world tabular data.  While elements of this approach have been explored separately (e.g., semantic embeddings in CARTE or real-world pretraining in TabuLa-8B), ConTextTab attempts a more balanced and efficient integration.  The choice to build upon TabPFN's efficient architecture rather than a full-blown LLM is a strategic one.  However, the architecture itself is not revolutionary. A significant portion is based on existing techniques, carefully assembled.

*   **Significance:** The significance comes from addressing a recognized limitation of previous table-native ICL methods. By incorporating semantic information, ConTextTab shows improved performance on datasets where that information is relevant (CARTE). It potentially opens the door to tabular ICL models that can better leverage the rich information in real-world tables. A key benefit is achieving this semantic understanding *without* the computational overhead of LLM-based approaches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the approach and its motivations.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, evaluating ConTextTab against strong baselines on a variety of benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components of the model.
    *   The analysis of performance as a function of dataset size is valuable.
    *   The CARTE benchmark performance demonstrably justifies the addition of semantic components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the method is novel in its combination of elements, it isn't fundamentally groundbreaking. It's an incremental improvement on existing architectures.
    *   The model still struggles with very large datasets compared to traditional methods (boosted trees) and AutoML. This suggests that there's more work to be done on scaling tabular ICL effectively.
    *   The ablation studies, while useful, don't always provide definitive answers. For example, the performance gains from weight sharing/not sharing and choice of embeddings are small and could be noisy.
    *   The reliance on T4 dataset creates an inherent potential for data contamination, even though the contamination studies indicated a low risk.
    *   The improvements on semantically poor datasets (where performance is similar to TabPFN) implies limited usefulness to datasets containing only numbers.

*   **Potential Influence:** ConTextTab is likely to influence future research in tabular ICL by highlighting the importance of semantic information and by providing a practical framework for integrating it into table-native architectures. It might encourage more exploration of data type-specific embeddings and training on real-world data. Also, it could steer the focus on creating new, more challenging, semantically-rich datasets.

*   **Score Justification:** While the paper presents a valuable contribution by integrating semantic awareness into a table-native tabular ICL framework, leading to performance improvements on semantically-rich datasets, its core architectural innovations are not revolutionary. It builds upon existing approaches in a well-engineered way.
